<a href="https://mathvault.ca/desmos-guide/#comment-3438"><time datetime="2020-11-26T14:17:33-05:00">November 26, 2020 at 2:17 PM</time></a></div></footer><div class="comment-content"><p>You can do this by making a slider on the graph.</p><p>Define the variable you want to use for the slider, eg &quot;a&quot;<br />
Set lower, upper limit and stepsize eg 0, 10, 0.001<br />
Now define a slideable point: (a,1) and check the label box<br />
The point should now appear in your graph. It might not show all the decimals but if you zoom in on the point to have more accuracy in positioning it, additional decimals will appear. I use this for sliders and switches (0-1). That way I can close the sidepanel and still cotnrol the graph.</p></div></article></li><li id="comment-3439" class="comment byuser comment-author-mvadmin bypostauthor odd alt depth-3"><article id="div-comment-3439" class="comment-body"><footer class="comment-meta"><div class="comment-author vcard">
<a class="thrive-comment-author-picture" href="https://mathvault.ca" rel="external nofollow"><img alt='' src='https://mathvault.ca/wp-content/litespeed/avatar/d96413892cebda856b0216ca7e7f9f76.jpg?ver=1781052280' srcset='https://mathvault.ca/wp-content/litespeed/avatar/eb51996c9e0ac86b012f99111ad59237.jpg?ver=1781052281 2x' class='avatar avatar-100 photo' loading='lazy' decoding='async'/></a>						<b class="fn"><a href="https://mathvault.ca" class="url" rel="ugc">Math Vault</a></b> <span class="says">says:</span></div><div class="comment-metadata">
<a href="https://mathvault.ca/desmos-guide/#comment-3439"><time datetime="2020-11-26T14:18:43-05:00">November 26, 2020 at 2:18 PM</time></a></div></footer><div class="comment-content"><p>Neat idea Frederik. Thanks for sharing!</p></div></article></li></ol></li></ol></li><li id="comment-5871" class="comment even thread-even depth-1 parent"><article id="div-comment-5871" class="comment-body"><footer class="comment-meta"><div class="comment-author vcard">
<a class="thrive-comment-author-picture" href="http://google" rel="external nofollow"><img alt='' src='https://mathvault.ca/wp-content/litespeed/avatar/7f16f869616daf7749b9db36619c2fc9.jpg?ver=1781051367' srcset='https://mathvault.ca/wp-content/litespeed/avatar/7d084c141e945450739cf5744d3e118c.jpg?ver=1781051368 2x' class='avatar avatar-100 photo' loading='lazy' decoding='async'/></a>						<b class="fn"><a href="http://google" class="url" rel="ugc external nofollow">William Toor Malueth</a></b> <span class="says">says:</span></div><div class="comment-metadata">
<a href="https://mathvault.ca/desmos-guide/#comment-5871"><time datetime="2022-06-24T04:11:58-04:00">June 24, 2022 at 4:11 AM</time></a></div></footer><div class="comment-content"><p>Great Job!! It&#039;s my first time to use it but extremely satisfied with such explanation. This wonderful to us the mathematics teachers/students.<br />
However, what isn&#039;t clear enough is how to copy your graph to other areas of work like Microsoft word.</p></div><div class="reply theme-button tcb-with-icon tcb-plain-text" data-button_layout="text_plus_icon" data-button-style="" data-button-size=""><a class="comment-reply-link tcb-button-link" rel="nofollow" href="#comment-5871" onclick="return addComment.moveForm( &#039;div-comment-5871&#039;, &#039;5871&#039;, &#039;respond&#039;, &#039;7485&#039; )" aria-label="Reply to William Toor Malueth"><span class="tcb-button-icon">

<a class="thrive-comment-author-picture" href="https://mathvault.ca" rel="external nofollow"><img alt='' src='https://mathvault.ca/wp-content/litespeed/avatar/d96413892cebda856b0216ca7e7f9f76.jpg?ver=1781052280' srcset='https://mathvault.ca/wp-content/litespeed/avatar/eb51996c9e0ac86b012f99111ad59237.jpg?ver=1781052281 2x' class='avatar avatar-100 photo' loading='lazy' decoding='async'/></a>						<b class="fn"><a href="https://mathvault.ca" class="url" rel="ugc">Math Vault</a></b> <span class="says">says:</span></div><div class="comment-metadata">
<a href="https://mathvault.ca/desmos-guide/#comment-5874"><time datetime="2022-06-24T16:25:15-04:00">June 24, 2022 at 4:25 PM</time></a></div></footer><div class="comment-content"><p>Hi William. Glad that you find the tool useful. If you&#8217;re merely looking to copy an unanimated graph to a word processor, you can either use the share icon on the upper right of the Desmos interface to export the graph as an image, or to simply take a screenshot (which can be done natively in both Mac and Windows).</p></div><div class="reply theme-button tcb-with-icon tcb-plain-text" data-button_layout="text_plus_icon" data-button-style="" data-button-size=""><a class="comment-reply-link tcb-button-link" rel="nofollow" href="#comment-5874" onclick="return addComment.moveForm( &#039;div-comment-5874&#039;, &#039;5874&#039;, &#039;respond&#039;, &#039;7485&#039; )" aria-label="Reply to Math Vault"><span class="tcb-button-icon">

from the site!">Math Vault</a> <script type='text/javascript'>( $ => {
	/**
	 * Displays toast message from storage, it is used when the user is redirected after login
	 */
	if ( window.sessionStorage ) {
		$( window ).on( 'tcb_after_dom_ready', () => {
			const message = sessionStorage.getItem( 'tcb_toast_message' );

			if ( message ) {
				tcbToast( sessionStorage.getItem( 'tcb_toast_message' ), false );
				sessionStorage.removeItem( 'tcb_toast_message' );
			}
		} );
	}

	/**
	 * Displays toast message
	 *
	 * @param {string}   message  - message to display
	 * @param {Boolean}  error    - whether the message is an error or not
	 * @param {Function} callback - callback function to be called after the message is closed
	 */
	function tcbToast( message, error, callback ) {
		/* Also allow "message" objects */
		if ( typeof message !== 'string' ) {
			message = message.message || message.error || message.success;
		}
		if ( ! error ) {
			error = false;
		}
		TCB_Front.notificationElement.toggle( message, error ? 'error' : 'success', callback );
	}
} )( typeof ThriveGlobal === 'undefined' ? jQuery : ThriveGlobal.$j );</script><script>window.MathJax = {
